## Closed cycle 140 — the ritual finds its voice, and the last two grounds find residents (2026-08-25)

> Both tracks APPROVED, and **Milestone 15 SHIPPED** — 423 was the last unchecked arc in it. 423 was filed
> as a prompt nudge and would have been bit-identical without a model, so the Designer split it in two and
> shipped the deterministic half to every device; the frozen 408/413/420 opener strings are now pinned
> byte-for-byte in a unit test. 500 cashed a comment `founding.ts` had been carrying for a cycle, and its
> own fallout is filed as BACKLOG-505 rather than left in a spec comment. The Structure Track's 500 pointer
> goes with the body text.

- [x] BACKLOG-423 [ai] Tic-flavored voice — a caught dino's reply is prompt-nudged by which ritual it was at (a pacer sounds restless, a fusser distracted), enrichment-on-top with the deterministic bashful/fond frame (408/413) unchanged under stub/fallback. Builds on 408 / 413 / 393.

- [x] BACKLOG-500 [infra] The grounds nobody lives on — CHARTER v7's third change reads "**the cast ships across the map**, not stacked in one zone: the roster carries a spawn zone, and every ground the player can walk to has life on it at boot." The roster it shipped spreads eight dinos as **5 bowl / 2 grove / 1 fernreach / 0 hollow / 0 ridge** — so two of the five grounds are exactly as empty at boot as all four were before the amendment, and the sentence that says otherwise is in the constitution. Everything a ground can hold (a plot, a landmark, a pile, a provider, a council, an upkeep bill, a mend errand) is inert on the Hollow and the Ridge from boot to save-death, and the Ridge is the *branch* — the one ground reached by a choice rather than by walking the line. Give the roster a residency invariant with a test that reads `zoneChain()` and asserts every ground has at least one resident, and seed whatever residents that costs. Note the tension to resolve rather than paper over: the bowl at five is the cast the 460 last-one floor, the huddle and the food scramble were all tuned against, so this is a *rebalance* of eight bodies (or a slightly larger roster), not a redistribution done blind. Builds on 486 / 474 / 460.

## Closed cycle 138 — the little path learns to wander, and the gate learns who is speaking (2026-08-23)

- [x] BACKLOG-421 [emergent] The ritual drifts — over many solitary stretches a dino's tic anchor slowly wanders its zone instead of pinning one tile, so its "little path" migrates and the ritual reads as a living habit, not a fixed loop. Builds on 405 / 421-none; 405. **SHIPPED cycle 138** — as a *haunt*, not as a slower pin. The filed premise was wrong: the anchor never pinned a tile, it was re-chosen from wherever the wander dropped the dino and thrown away by `resetTic`, so six stretches on one ground happened in six unrelated places. New `Haunt`/`Haunts` in `world/tic.ts` with `ticAnchorFor` / `driftHaunt` / `hauntSeed`: one worn tile per (dino, ground), returned to and nudged one tile per stretch, name-seeded so it never touches `world/rng.ts` and survives a reload. Beyond `HAUNT_RETURN_RANGE` (6) the old haunt is abandoned and a new one is laid underfoot — a habit you have wandered away from is a habit you have lost. Grief (414) still outranks it and deliberately leaves the haunt alone. `ticHaunts` additive save field; one-time memory + ticker beat at `HAUNT_DRIFT_NOTED` (4) drifts. +11 unit, +4 e2e.

- [x] BACKLOG-489 [core] The gate that was written for one door — cycle 133's finding, generalized. 485's first draft was correct in every hook and posted nothing, because 481's `checkCouncilCall` seeds silently on a ground's *first* recorded call — sound for a vote (an opening seating is not a turnover), fatal for a second source of the same event, and fatal **most reliably for the population that second source exists to cover**. Four freshness gates in this park now carry an implicit "…as decided by whatever was here first": the rumor freshness (222/233), the gratitude fade (251), one-visit-per-sorrow (226), the once-a-day discontent (471). Each will silence a later cause the day one is added. Give the pattern one shared seam — a gate that distinguishes *seeding* from *suppressing*, so a first record from a new source fires rather than seeds — port the four existing gates onto it, and leave a test per gate that adds a second cause and asserts it is heard. Nothing player-visible changes today; the point is that the next feature built on any of the four is not silently half-shipped. Builds on 485 / 481 / 471 / 251. **SHIPPED cycle 138** — scoped to the two gates that actually exist. Of the four this item names, 471 already fires on its first record, 251 is a ring-position window, and 226 has never been built (`sympathyVisit` carries a `ponytail:` note saying so). New pure `world/gates.ts`: `recordCall(log, key, cause, value)` keeps 481's rule and asks it of the **cause** rather than the ordinal, so an opening seating is silent because a council's first word is a seating — and a second authority on the same ground is heard. 481's work call and 487's spend call ported; 485's hand-rolled `!seeding || lean === call` deleted. **Reachable on a fresh save:** mend the Grove's founding ruin (488) and the ground now says who is deciding in the bill's place, where before it said nothing because the council's call happened to equal the bill's. +10 unit, +2 e2e.

- [x] BACKLOG-498 [infra] The save field nobody reloads — 422 added `catchWarmth` (the lifetime ceiling that stops being-found becoming farmable) beside `foodBanked`, restored it with the same `?? {}` idiom, and **nothing asserts it survives a reload**. QA found the gap and recorded it rather than counting the criterion covered, which is the right call — but it is a gap with a pattern behind it: the save's shape spec covers the *envelope*, and the per-field round-trip is left to whichever cycle happens to write an e2e that reloads. A field whose entire purpose is to survive a reload, and which is unobservable until somebody reloads, is exactly the one that will be dropped by a refactor and caught by nothing. Give `saveGame` a **field-coverage spec**: enumerate the interface's optional keys, assert each one that the scene writes is written and restored, and fail on a key the scene knows about that the round-trip does not. Then `catchWarmth` is covered, and so is the next one. Builds on 422 / 426 / 431. **SHIPPED cycle 138** — and it was already true when it was filed. `catchWarmth` was declared, written, and never parsed, so every reload refunded 422's lifetime ceiling; the item filed the night before turned out to be describing the field in front of it rather than a future one. `parseSave` now restores it, and `tests/unit/cycle-138-save-coverage.test.ts` reads the `SaveData` interface out of the source and fails on any declared key the parser does not hand back — verified by deleting one on purpose. +6 unit.

> ## Cycle 135 structure additions — a vote nothing can change (2026-08-20)
> 
> > The Structure Track sat at 3 open (below cap X=4), so one was brainstormed while picking 487. It is the

- [x] BACKLOG-492 [core] A vote nothing can change — with 487 landed, a ground's two calls are both decided by its council, and **neither can ever turn over on anything that happens in the park**. Every seat votes `providerWorkPriority(traits)` (energy ≥ 0.5) or `providerPriority(traits)` (agreeableness ≥ 0.5): a hard threshold on a single **name-seeded** axis (010), fixed at the moment the dino was named and untouched by every ledger this park keeps. A ground can starve for a season, watch its granary go derelict, lose its provider and seat three new dinos, and its politics are exactly the arithmetic of five birth-numbers — 484's term is a calendar over a constant, and 485's bill has to *override* the vote precisely because it cannot *persuade* it. Give a seat something it has lived to vote on: shade each vote by that dino's own experience of the ground it sits for — its hunger (371), its banked share (448), whether it was one of the mouths the reserve held short (471), whether it stood in a zone whose landmark came down (480) — as a bounded nudge across the 0.5 line rather than a replacement for the trait, so a warm dino that has gone hungry under a banking council can vote to feed and a ground's election can finally be *about* something. The seat's temperament stays the floor, exactly the way 043/187's capped drift shades a trait without erasing it, and an unlived (fresh-save) council is bit-identical to today's. The first time in this park's life that a decision answers to a history. Builds on 487 / 481 / 471 / 448 / 043.

  **call** the existing threshold functions on shaded traits rather than restating `>= 0.5`, so every
  463/473/481/487 spec stays green by construction, and an unlived seat is the pre-492 answer to the bit.

## Closed cycle 136 housekeeping — the operator block drains (2026-08-21)

> BACKLOG-493 shipped in cycle 135's operator pass and its section had no open items left, so the whole
> block moves here. The working backlog stays lean; every stage reads it.

- [x] BACKLOG-493 [core] The day nobody can reach — the default clock is 1x, so **an in-game day costs 24 real hours**, and every system in this park that fires on the day boundary is therefore unreachable in a normal session: the council's term (484), upkeep and disrepair (480), spoilage (455), crop growth, the dawn beat, the once-a-day discontent gate (471). Two full milestones of structure work sit behind this one number. `T` already toggles 60x (a 24-minute day) and the save carries the choice, so the *mechanism* is built and tested — what is missing is the default. **Measured 2026-08-20, so the next fire does not have to re-derive it:** flipping `WorldClock._scale` to 60 turns roughly ten specs red, and they split cleanly — `cycle-028-realtime` (x2), `smoke` (clock ticks), `cycle-002-daynight` (tint), `cycle-045-chorus` and `cycle-104-wake-hungry` (both "once per in-game day, and a fresh day re-arms it" — at 60x a fresh day arrives mid-test), `cycle-114-away-spoilage` (x2) and `cycle-029-away` (offline catch-up over a multi-day absence), plus a `controls-help` toggle race. The last group is the actual design work rather than spec bookkeeping: **what a "multi-day absence" means changes** when a day costs 24 minutes, and `away.ts`'s catch-up, the spoilage bleed and the homecoming digest all read that. Decide the semantics first, then move the constant. Builds on 105 / 455 / 480 / 484 / 471.
  *(shipped: cycle 135 operator pass. The world runs at two rates — `ACTIVE_SCALE` (60x, a 24-minute in-game
  day) while somebody is watching, `AWAY_SCALE` (1x, real time) the moment the tab is hidden, with the
  offline catch-up reading `AWAY_SCALE` rather than whatever rate the save was being watched at. Keeping the
  away rate at 1 is the load-bearing decision: `MAX_AWAY_DAYS = 7` still means seven **real** days, so
  nothing about being away changed meaning at all — only watching got faster, and two of the specs the
  cycle-135 measurement had listed as blockers passed unchanged as a result. The spec fallout was the
  interesting part: `cycle-045-chorus` and `cycle-104-wake-hungry` hardcoded wall milliseconds computed
  against 1x, with a comment saying "at 1x", and now say what they mean via `wallMsFor`.)*

## Closed items (cycle 136, 2026-08-21 — Milestone 15 "Somebody does it" opens: a catch that escalates + a ruin somebody mends)

- [x] BACKLOG-488 [core] Hands on the derelict — 480 made a landmark cost something to keep and gave disrepair a reversible cure, but the cure is arithmetic: `runUpkeep` patches the oldest derelict the instant the pile can spare `REPAIR_COST`, with no dino anywhere near it. Every other economy in this park is *performed* — a harvest is hauled (446), a unit is ferried across an edge by a body that remembers carrying it (447/451), a landmark is raised where a dino stood. Repair alone happens to a ground rather than in it. Make it a job: a resident of a ground carrying a derelict landmark (and, once 485 lands, a ground whose call has turned to gathering because of it) walks to that landmark, and the patch-up resolves *on arrival* rather than on the day tick — the fixer keeping a small "put the Grove's cairn back up" memory the way the courier keeps its pride. The pure arithmetic in `upkeep.ts` is unchanged; what moves is *who* triggers `repaired` and *where* they are standing when it happens. The first time in the park's life that a building is mended by somebody. Builds on 480 / 485 / 447 / 451.
  *(shipped: cycle 136 structure track, **Milestone 15 structure arc 1**. Delivered as written: `world/upkeep.ts` is untouched, and what moved is *who* triggers `repaired` and where they are standing when it happens. A new pure `world/mending.ts` holds the dispatch decision, the step budget and the words; `WorldScene` holds the errand and the walk, built on the 381 escort's shape — adjacency ends it, the budget is the safety valve, and the pile is debited **on arrival, never on dispatch**, so a failed errand costs the ground nothing. The spend goes through `runUpkeep(pile, 0, 1)`: zero standing landmarks means zero bill, so the only thing that call performs is the repair spend, by the same largest-kind rule upkeep has always used, through the exact function that has always done it — with a unit test pinning that it costs `REPAIR_COST` and lapses nothing, so a later `upkeep.ts` change cannot quietly make a mend charge a ground its bill as well. The live day pass now asks for the bill only (`derelict = 0`); the away catch-up keeps 480's full arithmetic, because nobody is watching an unattended park and there are no hands to send. **The founding change is why this is APPROVED and not filed.** `upkeep.ts` carried, in its own header, "a fresh park is inert" as a virtue; under CHARTER v7 that is the defect, so the park now boots with a fallen cairn in the Grove and the stone to raise it, on a ground the founding cast lives on, one edge from spawn. Two founding unit tests exist purely as **reachability pins** — the founding pile can afford the founding repair, and the ruin's ground has residents — either of which could break and take the whole 480/485/488 arc dormant again without another test going red. Two bugs caught in-fire: the walk moved into `stepMend()` so exactly one place moves the fixer and the spec hook drives production's own call, and a `foundingCleared` flag closed a race where a spec's clear could lose to `loadFromDb()`'s promise (the same ordering class as cycle 133's freshness gate). Additive save only — ordinary `cairns`/`stockpileByZone` entries, no version bump, and a restored save is never given a ruin. +19 unit, +7 e2e. (structure track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-420 [social] Caught again — greet a fond dino mid-tic (413) a second time in the same solitary stretch and its pleasure turns to playful teasing ("you again? spying on me?"), so a repeat catch reads different from the first warm one. Builds on 413 / 408.
  *(shipped: cycle 136 lore track. The catch has forked exactly two ways since cycle 89 and both are constant strings, so a player who found the same dino five times in one unbroken stretch of solitude got the identical opener five times. Now the **fond** reading climbs across one stretch — pleased, then playful teasing, then fond resignation — and floors there; a ninth catch is a third catch. **The unfond reading does not climb, and the flatness is the read**: a dino you have not befriended stays sheepish however often you find it, so the escalation is itself the tell that this one likes you. That is characterisation extracted from a constant nobody had to add. The tease is worded off `signatureAxis` — and off the *echoed* axis where a dino picked its ritual up from a friend (407), through a new `ticAxisFor` twin of `ticFor` — so the dino objects in the voice of the ritual you actually interrupted, not the one it was born with; a unit test asserts five distinct teases and five distinct resignations so a future reword cannot collapse them into the sameness the CHARTER calls a defect. The compatibility seam is the good part: `caughtOpener`/`caughtRegisterMemory` **call** `fondOpener`/`bashfulOpener`/`caughtMemory`/`fondCaughtMemory` rather than restating their text, pinned across every axis, which is why `cycle-088-caught-mid-tic` and `cycle-089-fond-caught` are green **unedited**. The per-stretch counter advances inside the `if (caught)` branch and nowhere else, so a cancelled greet cannot burn a register, and `resetTic` clears both the count and the per-register filed keys — being caught twice today does not make a dino permanently sardonic. Deliberately not persisted, exactly as `soloSteps` never was. +11 unit, +4 e2e. (lore track)*


## Closed items (cycle 135, 2026-08-20 — Milestone 14 "A ritual that spreads, and a government with a term" **SHIPPED** 🎉: two loners in sight of each other + the ground's other call goes to the council)

- [x] BACKLOG-487 [core] The other call goes to the council — 481 handed a ground's **work** priority (473) to its council by majority off the members' temperaments, the provider breaking ties. The ground's **spend** priority (463) — feed-the-hungry-first vs. bank-toward-a-granary, the older and more player-visible of the two calls, on the lens since 468 and in the cast's mouths since 469/470/471 — is still set unilaterally by whoever out-banks everyone else, and re-set silently on every handover (467). Run it through the same decision the work call now uses, so a ground's two calls are made the same way by the same seats, the handover beat (467) fires on a *vote* rather than one dino's temperament, and the discontent ticker (471) is finally answering a decision the ground actually made together. Reuses 481's tally wholesale; additive save. Builds on 481 / 473 / 463 / 467.
  *(shipped: cycle 135 structure track, **Milestone 14 structure arc 2 — the milestone SHIPPED**. 481 handed a ground's *labour* call to its council and left the *pantry* call with the provider on purpose, saying so in the source: "leaving 463 with the provider means the unchanged call sits beside the changed one as a live control." Four cycles later the control reported. The voted call had acquired a term (484), a turnover beat (484) and a feedback loop from the ground's own skyline (485); the unvoted one was still re-set unilaterally and silently by whoever out-banked everyone else, on every handover (467). **The item turned out to be smaller than the arc it closes, and that is the finding.** 481's counting loop had nothing to do with labour — it is "count the options, take the plurality, fall to the say and then to the top banker" — so the whole change is one generic (`councilMajority<T extends string>`) plus a mirror of an existing ladder. `councilWorkPriority` is now a one-line delegation to it and **its pre-487 spec cases are byte-identical and pass untouched**, which is the delegation's entire proof; writing the arithmetic a second time for `SpendPriority` would have been `standings.ts`'s (482) failure one layer down — two comparators that must agree forever and no test that they do. The generic is deliberately **stated for a plurality, not for two options**: both of today's calls are binary, but the tie-break law is written for a plurality, and a third option must not be the moment somebody re-derives what a tie means under pressure. Each seat votes `providerPriority(traits)` — the *same* agreeableness read the provider always used — so a council of one behaves exactly as that one dino did alone, and an e2e pins that as the compatibility control: a shipping five-dino park is bit-identical, which is why the interesting case had to grow a ground to six residents to reach at all. **The planned trap was real.** `checkCouncilCall`'s entry guard lets a *derelict landmark* open the door, because 485's lean is a labour concept; letting it open the pantry door too would have had the bill announcing a decision it does not touch. The shipped guard computes `seated` once and gates the two halves differently, commented, with `cycle-133-bill-call.spec.ts` passing unchanged. And the **declined** deviation is the better call, recorded as such: the plan asked for the two announce-blocks folded into one helper, and the Coder refused because they stop being the same shape once the lean threads through one — a helper taking a parameter meaningless to one caller is a worse seam than two honest blocks, and faking a shared gate here would make **BACKLOG-489** harder, not easier. That is the second time in three cycles that these freshness gates have asked to be generalized by somebody not in a position to do it properly. The handover beat (467) now fires on a *vote* rather than one dino's temperament, and the discontent ticker (471) is at last answering a decision the ground made together. No new save field — `spendPriorityByZone`'s stored values are the same two strings; only who decided them widened. +8 unit, +5 e2e. (structure track)*

This is the beat with no channel at all — two dinos, each far enough into its own solitude to have invented a ritual, each near enough to see the other at it, and neither one crosses. **It introduces no new number, and that is the design rather than thrift**: the only distance at which this can happen is the distance at which a ritual can form *and* another dino is visible, which is precisely the band 407 had to name for the opposite reason, so `kinTic` reads `watchingTic` and the unit spec pins the reuse (3 false / 4 true / 8 true / 9 false) rather than leaving it as a comment. `kinTic` is `watchTic`'s shape with exactly two things removed — the bond floor and the assumption that only one end is ticcing — which is why it reads as obviously right beside its sibling. **The bond asymmetry is the feature**: 407 demands a real friend because you cannot learn a stranger's ritual from across a field; 416 demands none because you can be glad they are out there anyway, and the e2e stages a pair *below* `ECHO_BOND_FLOOR` and proves both file regardless. A dino that falls into its ritual scuffs the spot; one that wanders across the mark while it is fresh floats 👣 and files a faint, *unnamed* trace. The anonymity is the design — 'someone was pacing here' is exactly as much as trodden grass can honestly say. **A first for the park: a place holding a memory of a dino**, where 126 cycles of memory (347, 362, 343) have only ever run the other way. New pure `world/traces.ts`: one live mark per pacer, freshness measured in the same world-steps 405's solitude counts in, zone-scoped, freshest-wins deterministically, never your own scuff, once-per-*event* (`by:at`) rather than per-tile so standing on a patch is one discovery. Transient like all tic state — no save change. Per cycle 125's lesson, the e2e asserts exactly one file and then re-scans and asserts still exactly one. Three limitations recorded: the organic path is hook-proven not observed, the memory is one fixed line with no temperament shading (the obvious follow-up), and the notice scan is the first new per-step cast-wide scan since the food-web pairing. +12 unit, +1 e2e. (lore track)*

Closed (`[x]` shipped / `[a]` abandoned) items moved out of BACKLOG.md to keep the working file lean. The closed log lives here too. Move items here when they close; never reopen from here (re-seed a fresh BACKLOG-NNN instead).

## Closed items (cycle 124, 2026-08-07 — Milestone 11 "A park you have to cross" **SHIPPED** 🎉: two who go together + both of the ground's calls, on the lens)

- [x] BACKLOG-370 [social] Lonely lean on the keeper — a loner (135) drifts to the glass front toward the keeper instead of a random edge when its keeper-friendship is high, so a dino with no dino-friends leans on you specifically; loneliness becomes a bid for the keeper's attention. Builds on 135 / 112.  *(shipped: cycle 125 lore track, **Milestone 12 lore arc 1**. Named by 135's own closing note in cycle 80 and left for 45 cycles. `edgeTarget` picks the wall nearest **the dino**, so the one dino with a deep keeper relationship and no dino relationships withdrew in whatever direction it happened to face — while every other movement system built since reads *some* relationship to decide where a body goes. At ≥ `LEAN_HEARTS` (4 of 10) a moping loner now walks to the wall the **keeper** is by. `keeperEdgeTarget` **delegates** to `edgeTarget` rather than reimplementing 'nearest of four walls', so the spec can cheaply assert the two agree on all 300 tiles of the grid. In-view only. Dormant on a fresh park **by construction** — every dino spawns at 0 hearts *and* as a loner. **The finding is a testing one:** the plan's reuse of 408's `resetTic` guard filed the memory six times (the ring's capacity), because `resetTic` tracks the *tic* stretch, which company breaks every few steps, while a loner at the wall is still waiting — now cleared in `checkLonerLift`. Caught only because the spec was tightened from `toBeLessThanOrEqual(1)` to `toBe(1)`: a 'no more than once' assertion cannot tell correct from never-happened. No save change. +5 unit, +5 e2e. (lore track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-456 [infra] Pinned piles vs. ambient gathering — several crossing e2e specs seed a zone pile, walk a dino across over ~10 `__stepWorld` calls, then assert an exact pile total; ambient gathering runs on those same steps, so a resource that spawns and is picked up mid-crossing re-banks into the pinned pile (`cycle-077-carry`) or drains it under the soft cap via an auto-crafted cairn and flips the carry decision (`cycle-097-carry-pressure`, ~1 run in 6 on clean HEAD — fixed cycle 107 by glutting a kind the zone's own recipe can't spend). A third instance surfaced cycle 108 with a different noun: `cycle-076-news-pull` drives two crossings over ~40 `__stepWorld` calls, ambient *meetings* mutate `bonds` on those same steps, and any dino that turns homesick mid-drive makes `pickMigrant` take its homesick first branch — so the spec's exact-identity assert (`__maybeMigrate() === 'Mossback'`) gets `Sunny` instead. That one is *also* latently nondeterministic independent of load, since the homesick pool is picked with `Math.random()`, so it wants a seam **and** a deterministic pick. All three are pre-existing and pass isolated, so they read as parallel-load flakes and hide real regressions. A **fourth** noun surfaced cycle 122, and the first that is not a pinned-pile assert: `cycle-121-work-priority` → "the work policy persists across a reload" read `null` after `page.reload()` under a full parallel run, passed 5/5 isolated, and a fresh full run was green including it — a reload racing the IndexedDB write under load, so the seam this item wants may need a *flush-and-settle* helper as well as an ambient hold. Give the pattern a proper seam: a dev hook to hold a zone's spawn/gather still for the length of a driven crossing (the `__pauseAmbient` idea, one level down), and move the remaining specs onto it. Builds on 431 / 429 / 329.  *(shipped: cycle 125 structure track, **Milestone 12 structure arc 1**. Promoted out of `## Infra` into the Structure Track on the cycle-124 verdict's standing recommendation and built **before** the milestone's fifth ground rather than after it. Read as a bug it is unfixable, because it is not one — it is **three mechanisms wearing one item number**. (a) 431's `__pauseAmbient` only ever gated the wall-clock *timers*; nothing ever gated the ambient work riding a `forceStep` a spec drives itself. New `ambientHeld` + `__holdAmbient`/`__releaseAmbient`/`__ambientHeld` skips exactly the pairwise meeting loop, `maybeSpawnResource` and `checkGather` — and nothing else, because every spec this exists for is *driving* movement, a crossing, needs, feeding or a plot. Deliberately a **second flag, not a widening**: `ambientPaused` is called by `boot()` for all 475 specs. (b) `pickMigrant`'s homesick tier was the last `Math.random()` in a pickable set → `homesick[0]`, the `richestNeighbor`/`unsettledNeighbor`/`hopToward`/`pondCompanion` rule. (c) `__flushSave` hands back the promise `void this.saveGame()` discards, closing the reload race that no amount of ambient holding would have fixed. **The evidence is which specs did not fail:** all four catalogued nouns passed in *both* full runs, where two of them failed on the cycle-123 run and the cycle-124 run off unrelated diffs; run 2 was 475/475. Two partials recorded: the homesick 20-call repetition was not constructible (no `pickMigrant` hook), and run 1 had two cold-boot timeouts in `boot()` that passed isolated. No production path sets `ambientHeld`; no save change. +12 e2e. (structure track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-360 [social] Pond pilgrimage — two pond-swap companions (346) may later cross to the grove *together* (a near-simultaneous migration) to revisit the place they bonded over, so shared-place friendship becomes shared travel, not just shared talk. Builds on 346 / 334 / 288.
  *(shipped: cycle 124 lore track, **Milestone 11 lore arc 3 — MILESTONE SHIPPED**. Small in code, large in kind: for 124 cycles every crossing mechanism the park had built — ambient (334), homesick (340), scarcity (450), hearsay (458), longing (362) — moved exactly one body, and the bond graph had never once had a say in *who travels with whom*. This is the first time it does. The precondition is a record that already existed and had never been read: 346 has filed `🌿 traded pond stories with <name>` into the memory ring since cycle 76, a durable, persisted, per-pair record of two dinos who bonded over a place, and nothing had ever asked it a question. New pure `world/together.ts`: `pondCompanion(leaderMemories, candidates)` finds the first candidate whose `pondSwapMemory(candidate)` the leader carries — **positional, not random**, with a test that calls it twenty times against two eligible companions, because BACKLOG-456 catalogues `Math.random` in a pickable set as *the* flake shape and this cycle declined to add a fifth noun to that item; `travelsTogether(dest, sharedZone, ...)` gates on the destination being the ground the pair bonded over, with the shared ground a **parameter** rather than a `GROVE_ID` constant, so a second shared-place bond later is a caller change and not a rewrite; `togetherMemory`/`togetherLine`/`togetherEvent`, and `TOGETHER_BOND = 2` deliberately *below* `POND_BOND` 3 — discovering you both know a place is the bigger beat, going back to it together the smaller, warmer follow-up, pinned by a test. **The design decision that made it safe:** it is a *companion pull*, not a second decision. The companion rides the destination `startMigration` fixed a moment earlier and never chooses one, so `pickMigrant` and all four destination reads (`scarcityDestOf`, `plentyDestOf`, `yearnDestOf`, `hopToward`) are untouched — which is why a feature hooked into the migration seam moved **no pinned migration spec**. `tryTogether` is called from the tail of `tryHomesick` and `scarcityMigrate` rather than from `maybeMigrate`, because the dev hooks enter through those two: there is no test-only path anywhere in this feature. Dormant on a fresh save by construction — a pond swap requires both dinos to have walked to the grove *and* met back home — and asserted so, by an e2e that drives the seam for the entire cast on a fresh park and gets `null` every time. **One limitation recorded rather than papered over:** the pull fires only when a crossing is already bound for the grove, so a bonded pair can go a long while without travelling together. That is the arc's own reading (they go back when one of them was going anyway) and it is exactly what keeps the feature out of the destination logic — but rare-by-design is worth saying out loud rather than rediscovering later and calling it a bug. **No save-shape change at all**: the memory ring and `bonds` are both already persisted. Dev hook `__together`. +11 unit, +3 e2e. **Milestone 11 lore arc 3 ✅ — MILESTONE SHIPPED.** (lore track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-477 [core] Both of the ground's calls, on the lens — 468 put a zone's spend priority on the zone-map lens beside its prosperity tier. Once a ground decides a second thing (work priority, 473), the lens box has to carry two governance reads without becoming a wall of glyphs: fold the spend and work calls into one compact per-zone governance line with a legend the `[?]` panel explains, so a player can read *how each ground has chosen to live* at a glance rather than learning two unrelated icons. The generalization beat 473 makes necessary — a third call later is then a row, not a redesign. Builds on 468 / 473 / 463 / 425.
  *(shipped: cycle 124 structure track, **Milestone 11 structure arc 3 — MILESTONE SHIPPED**. The rare item whose success is measured by what the diff *removes*. The lens box's prosperity line had accreted five independent reads — tier, decline arrow, harvest tally, spend glyph, work glyph — of which two were the same *kind* of fact, appended by the cycles that invented them: 468 hung 🍽️/🏦 on the end of that line when there was one governance call and the end of a line was a fine place for it, and 473 added 🧺/🧱 by copying it. A third call would have copied it again, and nothing anywhere in the game had ever told the player what any of the four glyphs meant. Both of cycle 123's closing stages flagged the line independently — the Validator withheld a crowding glyph so it would not become the sixth read, and the Artist fire recorded the same judgement from the art side. Appended to `world/governance.ts` (not forked into a UI module — the glyph functions already lived there): `GovernanceOption`/`GovernanceCall` descriptors, `SPEND_CALL` (pantry) and `WORK_CALL` (labour), and `GOVERNANCE_CALLS` as the ordered table; `governanceLine(values)` folds a ground's calls into one row in table order and `governanceLegend()` renders the same table for the `[?]` panel — **derived from the same data, so the legend cannot drift from the glyphs it explains**, which is the half that makes the item worth doing (a glyph a player can't decode is decoration). A third call is now a literal new entry with no function body changing, asserted by a test that passes an extra value and gets an unchanged row. `spendGlyph`/`workGlyph` keep their bodies and their tests unamended, with a new case pinning the descriptors against both so the table and the two shipped readers can never disagree. The `null` seam holds: no calls set → empty string → nothing added to the box, so a young park's map is exactly as it was. **The placeholder is the design call and its character is the finding.** A ground with a spend policy and no work policy must not render a bare glyph the player reads as position one of two, so an unmade call renders a placeholder and the row stays position-stable — but `·`, the obvious pick, is *already drawn in this very box* by 474's `'· unsettled ·'` badge, on three of four grounds in a fresh park. A new glyph colliding with an existing read on the same panel is worse than no glyph; it is `▫`. It surfaced only because the spec asserts a fresh park contains **none** of the row's glyphs — the sort of negative assertion that usually feels redundant. Also: the row is suppressed entirely for an unsettled ground, not in the criteria and confirmed correct on 474's own rule that an unsettled box replaces its read rather than annotating it. `helpLines()` appends the legend; `drawZoneMap` drops both glyphs off the prosperity line and draws the row below it, `boxH` 92 → 104. `zoneMapModel` deliberately **unchanged** — this item changes the rendering, not the model, which is why the cycle-117 and cycle-121 lens specs passed unamended. New dev hooks `__zoneMapText` (draws on demand and returns the *rendered* label text, so the e2e proves the drawn row rather than the model) and `__helpText`. No save change. +6 unit, +2 e2e. **Milestone 11 structure arc 3 ✅ — MILESTONE SHIPPED.** (structure track)*

## Closed items (cycle 123, 2026-08-06 - Milestone 11 "A park you have to cross" arc 2 on both tracks: homebody or wanderer + what a ground can hold)

- [x] BACKLOG-361 [pokemon] Grove regulars in the book — the collection book tracks how many times each dino has crossed to the grove ("been to the pond 4×"), making homebody-vs-wanderer a legible standing rather than something you only feel. Builds on 334 / 021.  *(shipped: cycle 123 lore track, **Milestone 11 lore arc 2**, taken at its **generalized** reading - the fifth grove-era item in five cycles to need that treatment (343, 364, 362, 347, 361), which is now a pattern worth naming rather than a coincidence: items written when the park was one place encode the park being one place. The item asked for a per-destination grove tally; at four grounds the honest fact has **two dimensions**, and shipping only one would have been backwards. New pure `world/wandering.ts`: `Crossings` (dino -> lifetime arrivals) is the one thing kept, incremented at **both** arrival seams - in `crossDino` unconditionally (a walked crossing always lands on a linked neighbour and can never be a same-zone move) and in `relocate` inside its `from !== destZoneId` guard (`__migrate` can be told to send a dino where it already stands, and that is not a journey), an asymmetry stated in a comment rather than left to be rediscovered; `originOf(seen)` = `seenZones[name][0]`, already the first thing the park ever writes about a dino, so the item needed **no** new travel record beyond the counter; `reachOf(seen, origin)` derives the greatest hop-distance from the origin through 475's `hopsBetween` on every book open - never stored, because a stored `farthest` rots silently the day a link changes (the 449 lesson, re-argued in 475's own header the night before); an unreachable id in `seen` is **skipped**, not counted as 0 hops; `WANDERER_REACH = 2` is a *reach* threshold rather than a crossing count, so pacing back and forth can never earn the standing; `wanderStanding` does not consult reach at all when `crossings === 0` (a dino that has never left is a homebody however the map is drawn - pinned by a test that fabricates a reach of 99); `wanderBookLine` names the ground a homebody has never left and carries both numbers otherwise. **The design's whole claim, verified in-game by QA and not only in a unit test:** Sunny takes four shuttles between two adjacent grounds and stays `a rambler - 4 crossings, 1 ground out`; Twitch takes three and reads `a wanderer - 3 crossings, 3 grounds out`. Fewer journeys, higher standing. On a crossing count alone - the obvious build, and what the item text asked for - Sunny would outrank Twitch. Deliberately a **standing, not a beat**: no bubble, no ticker, no memory, because five beats already contend at the crossing instant (339/451/452/457, and 347 from one cycle earlier) and a sixth would have made one of them silently rarer. Optional `BookRow.wander`, rendered with the travel lines. Additive `crossings` save field on the `harvestedByZone` guard shape, no version bump, **no back-fill** - a save that never counted reads every dino a homebody rather than inventing journeys nobody watched, asserted. Dev hook `__crossings`. +27 unit, +6 e2e. **Milestone 11 lore arc 2.** (lore track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-476 [core] What a ground can hold — migration reads pull (prosperity, food store, 450) and push (hollowing, 460) but nothing ever reads *fullness*: a rich ground can absorb the entire cast and the only thing that stops it is the 460 floor on the ground being emptied. Give each zone a derived **carrying capacity** off its terrain descriptor (`ZONE_TERRAIN`, 449) and let crowding past it damp that ground's appeal and lift its residents' own leave-lean, so the chain settles into a distribution instead of a stampede toward whichever ground is currently richest. The structural counterweight 450/460 has been missing since the day it shipped, and it only became visible at four grounds. Builds on 449 / 450 / 460 / 428.  *(shipped: cycle 123 structure track, **Milestone 11 structure arc 2**. The Structure-smith's framing is the sharpest read in the cycle: **five accelerative systems, one brake, and the brake is on the wrong end.** 450 sends mouths toward plenty, 460 hurries the stragglers out of the ground they leave, 458 spreads word of the richest ground ahead of the bodies, 362 calls them back to grounds they miss, and 475 - shipped the night before - let all of it reach clear across the chain; the only counterweight in the set, `ZONE_FLOOR`, protects the ground being *emptied*. Nothing had ever protected the ground being *filled*. Invisible at three grounds; real the moment a pull could recruit a dino two hops away into a pile-up it cannot see. New pure `world/capacity.ts`, with capacity **derived** from `ZONE_TERRAIN` rather than tabled beside it - a per-zone capacity number would have been the second table 449 exists to prevent, re-introduced for the sake of four integers; a fifth ground gets a capacity the day it gets a terrain function. `livableTiles` counts **grass only**, and that choice is load-bearing: counting every non-water tile puts the four grounds within 6% of each other (294/288/278/290) and the feature comes out uniform - a system that exists without saying anything - where grass gives 294/248/226/250 and makes the Fernreach, all creek and scrub, the tight ground. `zoneCapacity` = `max(1, ceil(livable / TILES_PER_HEAD))`, floored so no ground is ever made uninhabitable by arithmetic: bowl 5, grove 5, Fernreach 4, Hollow 5. `isCrowded` strictly `>`. `crowdedAppeal` damps once per surplus mouth and is an **exact identity** when uncrowded (`toBe`, not `toBeCloseTo`) - and it folds into the appeal *number* rather than sitting above it as a tier, deliberately the opposite of 474's frontier call and documented beside it: 474 needed a tier because `zoneAppeal`'s two readers wanted opposite signs from it (`richestNeighbor` asking where to go, `poorestResidents` asking who leaves); crowding's two readers want the **same** sign, because a crowded ground is honestly both a worse place to arrive at and a likelier place to leave. When both readings agree, the honest home for the effect is the number. `CROWDED_MIGRATE_DAMP` 0.3 reuses 460's resist lever, and a ground both crowded and declining takes the **weaker** of the two holds (`Math.min`, pinned symmetric so a future divergence cannot silently pick the stronger). **The calibration is the feature and was treated as such:** the knob is set against the founding state, not a feeling - five dinos, bowl capacity 5, strict `>`, so the park boots *at* capacity and the whole system is dormant on a fresh save. The code plan wrote down in advance that a moved migration spec meant the knob was wrong and **the spec must not be amended**; the Coder proved `capacity.ts` in isolation (16 tests) before touching the scene, and **no pinned spec moved - no test file outside this cycle's two new ones was touched.** That is Milestone 10's finding read the right way round: M10 learned that a suite full of hard-coded assumptions moves when a general system meets a new case, and here the suite standing perfectly still is the *evidence* that the ceiling is genuinely inert at the founding state. Lens glyph deliberately withheld - a loose crowding icon beside the prosperity tier and the down-arrow is exactly the accretion 477 exists to fold up, and adding it now would make 477 a redesign instead of a row. **No save change**: capacity is derived, crowding is read live. Dev hooks `__zoneCapacity`, `__crowded`. +16 unit, +6 e2e. **Milestone 11 structure arc 2.** (structure track)*

## Closed items (cycle 122, 2026-08-05 - Milestone 11 "A park you have to cross" opens: still full of the place it left + distance on the chain)

- [x] BACKLOG-347 [emergent] Grove-struck idle - a dino freshly back from another ground floats a wistful keepsake bubble for a short window after returning, so you can read who's just come home from over there.  *(shipped: cycle 122 lore track, **Milestone 11 lore arc 1**, and taken at its **generalized** reading: queued at cycle 75 as "back from the grove ... in the bowl", which described the whole park at the time and would have been a hard-coded case of four now. The beat keys on whichever ground the dino left - the same reframing 343, 364 and 362 each took, and the direct application of Milestone 10's finding that the code generalizes while the assertions don't. New pure `world/struck.ts`: `CameFrom` (dino -> the ground it last crossed **out** of) as its own small record, the `LeftDays` precedent; `KEEPSAKE`/`keepsakeGlyph` one glyph per ground (bowl 🌾, grove 🌿, Fernreach 🍂, Hollow 🌫) with a `'🌿'` fallback so a new zone can never blank the beat - a fifth ground is a row; `isStruck(rolls, from)` reads **341's tenure** as its clock rather than persisting a second counter, since tenure already counts rolls in the current zone and already resets on every crossing; `STRUCK_MARK`/`struckMemory`/`struckLine`/`struckEvent`/`struckBookLine`, with a test pinning that the memory carries neither `PLENTY_TOKEN` nor the grove-news phrase (the re-spread hazard 362's header names). Wired at **both** crossing seams: the memory files on arrival and rides `recall -> recentMemory -> greet`, but the glance back is floated a roll later by `bumpTenures` and deliberately **not** at the crossing instant, where four beats already contend (339 look-around, 451 courier, 452 homecoming, 457 greener-ground). The ticker logs once per crossing, not once per float. `BookRow.struck` reads **live** state (`cameFrom` + tenure) rather than parsing the memory ring - the ring holds the memory long past the window, so a parse would have stranded `just back from ...` on the dossier forever, which is exactly the cycle-56 gratitude wart (251) caught before shipping it a second time; the code plan argued the deviation in writing before making it. **A homecoming is not struck**: 452 restores tenure to `SETTLE_ROLLS`, so a dino walking back into the ground it belongs to reads false - chosen, not discovered, and pinned by a spec so the 🏡 beat keeps that moment alone. Additive `cameFrom` save field with a string-valued-object guard, no version bump, absent -> `{}`. Dev hooks `__struck`, `__homeZone`. +18 unit, +8 e2e. (lore track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-475 [core] Distance on the chain - a derived hop-distance table off the adjacency graph, so a pull toward a ground that isn't adjacent becomes a step *toward* it and the demand read prefers the nearest qualifying ground.  *(shipped: cycle 122 structure track, **Milestone 11 structure arc 1**. The Structure-smith read the code before picking the item and came back with something sharper than the item text: the ferry (447) and the demand read (438) are one-hop **by construction**, which is defensible - but the two migration *pulls* shipped in the previous two cycles were one-hop **by discard**. `plentyDestOf` (458) and `yearnDestOf` (362) each computed what a dino wanted and then returned `null` when it wasn't adjacent, so a dino standing in the bowl could not miss, or act on news of, the Hollow. Correct at three grounds where every zone bordered the middle; wrong from the day the fourth landed. New pure `world/distance.ts`, derived from `ZONE_LINKS` by breadth-first walk rather than tabled beside it (the 449 lesson - no second table to fall out of sync): `hopDistances`, `hopsBetween`, `hopToward(from, to)` (the neighbour on a shortest path; neighbours walked in link order so it is deterministic without a sort and with no `Math.random()` anywhere near a migration decision - BACKLOG-456), `nearestQualifying`. Named `hopToward` because `movement.stepToward` was already in scope. **`hopToward(a, b) === b` for any adjacent `b`** is the entire back-compat argument and the spec asserts it over *every* link in `ZONE_LINKS`, so "every pre-475 caller is byte-identical" is a proof rather than a claim. The multi-hop walk persists nothing: each migration roll re-reads the pull and takes one more step, so crossing the park is the existing per-roll decision applied again, and a dino that changes its mind en route changes course. `zoneWant` (438) widened from neighbours to the whole chain, ranked nearest-first, with 438's greater-harvest rule kept as the tie-break among equally-distant growers and its 0 floor intact; `ZoneWant.hops` added for 477 to read. **The finding:** the code plan predicted that widening `yearnedZone`'s candidate set changes which ground a dino misses and told the Coder to verify rather than assume - the real defect sat a step downstream. `seedYearning` and the `💭 ... misses ...` ticker both read `yearnDestOf`, which after 475 returns the *next hop*; shipped as written, a dino that missed the Hollow would have filed, said and shown "misses The Grove", a lie in the memory ring, the book and the ticker that no unit test of either pure module could have caught because both modules were correct. Fixed by splitting each pull into a **target** (what it wants - every word reads this) and a **dest** (where it steps - every move reads this), with an e2e asserting the positive *and* the negative. No save change. Dev hooks `__yearnTarget`, `__plentyTarget`, `__plentyDest`, `__scarcityMigrate`, `__setHarvests`. +23 unit, +7 e2e. (structure track)*

## Closed items (cycle 121, 2026-08-04 — Milestone 10 "A fourth ground, and the first feet on it" SHIPPED 🎉: a ground you come to miss + the ground's second decision)

- [x] BACKLOG-362 [emergent] A ground you come to miss — a dino long back from a ground it has stood on files a faint yearning that re-primes it to return, so a place can be missed and not merely visited.  *(shipped: cycle 121 lore track, **Milestone 10's final arc — M10 SHIPPED**. Queued at cycle 79 as the grove-specific "Grove-struck homesickness" and generalized here to any ground, the same reframing 343 and 364 took. The observation underneath it: **every** migration bias this park has ever shipped is a push — scarcity empties the poorest ground (450), a hollowing one hurries its stragglers out (460), a dry pantry sends a mouth toward a fuller one (457/458), and even homesickness (340) is a pull toward a *dino*, not a place. Nothing had ever made a dino leave good ground because somewhere else got under its skin. New pure `world/yearning.ts`: `LeftDays` (dino → zone → the in-game day it last crossed **out**) is deliberately its own small record rather than a widened `SeenZones` — folding a `leftDay` into 364's map would have touched a shipped parse guard and every 364 spec to buy nothing; `markLeft`/`clearLeft` (arriving ends the longing — you cannot miss where you are standing); `yearnThreshold(traits)` → `CURIOUS_YEARN_DAYS` 2 for `curiosity ≥ 0.5` else `YEARN_DAYS` 3, so temperament colours the *strength* of the pull and never whether it exists; `yearnedZone` returns the reachable neighbour left **longest** ago past that threshold, walking `ZONE_LINKS` order with a strict `<` so the first neighbour wins a tie — the `richestNeighbor`/`unsettledNeighbor` precedent, and no `Math.random()` anywhere near a migration decision (BACKLOG-456); `yearnMemory`/`yearnLine`/`yearnEvent`/`yearnedFor`/`yearnBookLine`, the standing read off the **live memory ring** most-recent-first like `taughtCount`, so the book tracks the current longing rather than an outgrown one. Wired at both crossing seams and into **both halves** of migration: a new `pickMigrant` tier strictly below plenty-primed and strictly above the scarcity fallback (every pinned pick above it byte-identical), and a `scarcityDestOf` override that sends the migrant to the ground it misses rather than the richest neighbour — explicitly **not** tagged `'scarcity'`, so 457's greener-ground beat stays silent whatever the appeal maths say. `seedYearning` rides the migration cadence inside the `ambientPaused` guard so the 431 seam still holds still. Optional `BookRow.yearn`. Additive `leftDays` save field, object-of-objects-of-finite-numbers guard, no version bump, **no back-fill** — a ground you were never recorded leaving cannot yet be missed, so a fresh park is completely silent (asserted). **One finding, caught by the e2e and not by the unit tests:** the instant `relocate` path stamped the departure clock *after* `setZone`, recording the ground the dino had just arrived in and then immediately clearing it — a perfect no-op living in the order of two calls in the scene rather than in any pure read. `const from` is read one line earlier now, with a `from !== destZoneId` guard. Dev hooks `__leftDays`/`__yearnDest`/`__seedYearning`. +10 unit, +7 e2e. **Milestone 10 lore arc 3 ✅ — MILESTONE SHIPPED.** (lore track)*
- [x] BACKLOG-473 [emergent] The ground's second decision — a provider-set **work priority** (gather-first vs. build-first) the ambient gathering and building hooks read, so a ground's provider shapes not only how its store is spent but what its residents put their backs into.  *(shipped: cycle 121 structure track. 463 gave a provider one call and Milestone 9 spent five cycles making that one call visible, transferable and lived — but one decision is a setting, not a system. Three `if` chains collapsed, not two: `zoneTileAt`, `zoneWaterTile`, and `zoneTint` (which the item text hadn't counted). One `ZoneTerrain` descriptor per zone in a `ZONE_TERRAIN` table beside `ZONES`/`ZONE_LINKS` holds the tile rule, the tint and the landmark; all three dispatchers become one-line lookups keeping their **exact** signatures, so `atWater`, `drawFloor` and `needTargetFor` were untouched. All six per-zone functions (`groveTileAt`/`fernreachTileAt`/`bowlTileAt`/`grovePondTile`/`bowlPondTile`/`fernreachCreekTile`) stay exported unchanged — the landmark helpers take different argument subsets, so the table wraps them rather than editing three functions with four importers each — and the four existing terrain/pond test files plus `world/arrival.ts` needed no edit. **`WorldScene.ts` needed zero edits**, not even the one import line the plan budgeted: the only consumer of all three dispatchers never noticed they were rewritten, which is the proof the generalization is real. **The landmark was deliberately NOT derived from the rule** (the Structure-smith's criterion 2, overruled in the design with reasons written down): the three landmarks aren't reproducible by any single scan-and-centre rule — the grove's pond centre rounds *up* from its x-range, the Fernreach's creek rounds *down* — so deriving would have silently moved two of three tiles and broken byte-identical behavior, the higher bar. The handoff's own stated fallback was taken (explicit field, one authoring site per zone instead of two) and the anti-drift shipped as a **mechanism**: a table-driven invariant asserting every declared landmark reports `'water'` under its own zone's rule, iterating `Object.entries(ZONE_TERRAIN)` so a fourth row is covered the day it's added — retiring the hand-maintained "kept in sync with the water block in `groveTileAt`" comments that cycle 105's verdict had already flagged as a hazard. Acceptance was a test-only fourth zone getting ground, tint, landmark and working `atWater` with zero production edits. Zero player-visible change by design; no new e2e on purpose (the two existing terrain specs staying green unmodified is the proof). +11 unit.)*

## Closed log

### Cycle 77 — 2026-06-24
- BACKLOG-329 shipped (structure track) — Carry between zones: the first trade route. Per-zone resources (314) and per-zone stockpiles (328) gave each zone its own economy, but the two were sealed — a pile only ever grew from local gathering, so a branch banked in the grove could never reach the bowl. This cycle a *visible* crossing (334) moves one banked resource: when a dino walks to its zone edge and `crossDino` flips its home zone, it ferries one resource from the pile it left into the pile it entered. The pick is a pure deterministic helper — `pickCarry(src,dest)` takes the most-stocked kind the destination can still accept (skips a kind the dest is at cap for, null when nothing can move), and `takeResource` removes it; WorldScene applies the move via `takeResource` + the existing `bankResource`, so a carry is conserved (source −1 / dest +1) and lossless (an empty source or a fully-capped destination carries nothing). The instant `__migrate`/`relocate` teleport path is untouched — carry rides only the watchable crossing — so every cycle-068/069/071 migration & zone spec stays green by construction. No save change (`stockpileByZone` already round-trips). 7 unit + 1 e2e. Directed (needs-based) carry is 356, two-way edge barter is 358.
- BACKLOG-346 shipped (lore track) — Pond-swappers: the grove becomes common ground. The grove arc had each dino discover the pond alone — arrival (339), the news that travels (342), the pull it exerts on the un-traveled (345). This cycle two travelers recognize each other: when two dinos who have *both* set foot in the grove (`groveVisited`) meet in the bowl, they trade pond notes — a small shared-place bond (`POND_BOND=3`, under the sky's shared-wonder) and a `🌿 traded pond stories with <other>` memory each. It's the grove's version of stargazing companions (288), built on the exact same spines: a pure `pondSwap(visited,a,b)` predicate + `pondSwapMemory(other)` in groveword.ts, and a `pondSwapBeat` that runs in `converse` after the sympathy/grateful block — additive and independent, so the cold/warm/relief/grove/gossip cascade is byte-identical when it doesn't fire. The one trap the design named: a swap memory must not read as fresh grove news, or a swapper would re-broadcast the pond to a third dino; `pondSwapMemory` is unit-pinned clear of `GROVE_NEWS_TOKEN`, and a swap-only holder spreads nothing groveward. No save change, NPCBrain untouched. 5 unit + 1 e2e. The book tie (353), the temperament-coloured swap line (354), and the stronger told-to-your-face pull (355) are the follow-ups.

### Cycle 73 — 2026-06-23
- BACKLOG-334 shipped (structure track) — Visible zone crossing: the journey to the grove is something you can watch now. Cycle 72 (333) made migration *frequent* at realtime, but `relocate` still teleported the migrant to a random far-zone tile — the dino just blinked out of the bowl. This cycle the ambient `maybeMigrate` roll, instead of teleporting, marks a dino "crossing"; a new `forceStep` branch (below inspect/respond, above food/huddle — a committed journey ignores snacks but a startle can pre-empt) walks it one tile per step toward its zone's linked edge (the bowl links east, the grove west — the same edges the keeper crosses), via three pure, unit-tested helpers in `zones.ts` (`migrationStepTarget`/`atMigrationEdge`/`crossEntryTile`). On reaching the edge, `crossDino` flips its home zone and drops it one tile in from the *opposite* edge of the new zone. Because cross-zone rendering is already gated by home zone (274), you see a bowl→grove migrant walk to the east wall and vanish as it leaves your view, and a grove→bowl one appear at the east edge and amble in. The deterministic `__migrate(name,zone)` hook keeps its instant teleport, so every cycle-068/069/071 migration spec + the save-restore path are byte-identical; the `migrating` Set is transient, no save change. `__startMigration`/`__migrating` hooks drive + observe the walk. 7 unit + 2 e2e. The grove-arrival reaction (a "first time across" beat) is the follow-up 339.
- BACKLOG-181 shipped (lore track) — Sleep murmurs: the night den has an inner life now. The cast packs the den at dusk (171) and you can sit and watch it at realtime, but five huddled dinos read identically. This cycle a huddling, in-view dino occasionally floats a quiet 💭 sleep-line drawn from its strongest memory of the day — Rex dreams of the favorite it ate, a cold-slept one mutters about the frost — so the sleeping pile becomes a place where each dino's day echoes back in its own half-formed thought. The core is a pure `world/murmur.ts` (no Phaser, no AI import): `pickMurmurMemory` takes the freshest memory, `murmurLine` wraps it as `💭 …fragment…` and strips the leading event glyph so the dream reads as a thought, not a copied log line (a memoryless dino dozes `💭 …zzz…`). `maybeMurmur` at the `forceStep` tail shows it on a sparse roll (`MURMUR_CHANCE=0.2`) for a huddling, in-view dino — an awake or out-of-zone dino stays silent — reusing `recall`/`showBubble`/`isHuddling`/`inView`. The deterministic line is the shipped path, so the den dreams with **no model** and the `NPCBrain` boundary stays intact (murmur.ts imports no backend); murmurs are ephemeral (no save). The LLM-coloured murmur was deliberately deferred (no AC needed it; it'd mean a buildMessages change + a governed night-time brain call) to the 181 follow-up thread (335 overheard, 336 in the book, 337 shared dreams). `__murmur`/`__forceMurmur` hooks. 5 unit + 2 e2e.

### Cycle 72 — 2026-06-22
- BACKLOG-333 shipped (structure track, operator-reported) — Realtime liveliness: the bowl was nearly static and the grove never filled, and the cause was a mismatch the realtime pivot (105) left behind. Wander ran every 5 *in-game* minutes and migration was capped to ≤1 per *in-game day* — both fine at the old 60× feel, but at the 1× realtime default an in-game minute is 60 real seconds and an in-game day is 24 real hours, so the park stepped once per ~5 real minutes and a dino crossed zones at most once per real day. This cycle moves both off the in-game clock: `forceStep` runs on a real-time timer (`WANDER_STEP_MS`, 3 s) so the cast mills about at a watchable pace whatever the time scale, and migration is paced by a real-time cooldown (`MIGRATE_COOLDOWN_MS`, 60 s) via a pure `cooldownReady` instead of the in-game-day cap, so dinos drift into the grove every few real minutes. `forceStep`'s body and the deterministic `__migrate` hook are untouched (so every gather/sky/zone spec that steps via `__stepWorld` stayed green), and there's no save change. The visible cross-zone *walk* (today's migration still teleports the dino to a random far-zone tile) is the follow-up 334. 3 unit + 1 e2e; the park is alive at 1× now.
- BACKLOG-325 shipped (lore track) — Lingering lift: the recovery flourish (318) gets a tail. 318 made a dino flash a brightened version of its signature quirk the instant a funk cleared; 325 keeps it perky for a short window after — `liftMood` stamps a per-dino real-time window (8 s) and the idle render shows the brightened glyph for a wandering, in-window dino before it settles back to its plain quirk. A sulk still wins the glyph (mood shading 310 takes precedence), so the perk only reads on a genuinely recovered dino. Pure render + a transient window, no save, no model; smooth decay (330) and a per-temperament window (327) are the follow-ups. 1 e2e + the `__lifted`/`__liftMood` hooks.

### Cycle 71 — 2026-06-22
- BACKLOG-318 shipped (lore track) — Mood lifts the motion: the recovery twin of cycle 70's mood-shaded fidget (310). Cycle 70 made a funk read in motion — a sulking dino mopes (😒) above its head — but when the funk cleared, the only signal was the glyph quietly vanishing. Now recovery reads in motion too: make up with a sulking dino (the 125 repair greet) or warm a cold-funked one (the 184 thaw) and it throws a brief brightened flourish of its *signature* quirk — `🐾✨`, `🤸✨` — a beat of its own body language coming back, parallel to the repair/warm bubble. The cut is a pure one-liner (`reliefFlourish(p)` = the signature glyph + a sparkle) reused through the existing `flashFeed` channel at the two recovery seams that already existed; no new bubble text, no affinity change, no save, no model. The lingering-perkier afterglow (325) and the prickly-slower flourish (327) are deferred. 2 unit + 1 e2e; 719 unit / 228 e2e green, no flake. The bowl shows its dinos bouncing back, not just stopping sulking.
- BACKLOG-314 shipped (structure track) — Zone-aware resource spawn: the grove grows its own gathering now. Resources lived in a single global slot that only ever rolled in the keeper's zone, so the grove — walkable (143), terrained (294), inhabited (274), zone-scoped (308) — still never grew anything to gather while you stood in the bowl. This cycle the resource goes per-zone: each inhabited zone holds its own slot and rolls on its own cadence (a pure `occupiedZones` drives the loop), ages it so it's past its fetch-grace and ready the moment you cross, and runs gather/fetch/visibility per zone. Cross into the grove and there's a branch waiting where there was nothing before; the bowl's own resource is right where you left it. The 274/308 gate is unchanged — a resource is still gathered only by a dino in the keeper's active zone — so the grove's waiting resource is picked up the moment you arrive. `__resource()`/`__spawnResource()` stayed backward-compatible (active-preferred, default zone = active), so every prior resource/craft/zone spec (062/064/065/066/069) stayed green untouched. No save change (the resource is transient). Per-zone *stockpile* (banking is still one shared pile) is 328; carrying resources across a crossing is 329. 4 unit + 1 e2e; the two zone economies finally exist.

### Cycle 70 — 2026-06-22
- BACKLOG-310 shipped (lore track) — Quirk shaded by feeling: the signature idle fidget now reads a dino's *right-now mood*, not only its temperament. For four cycles the `fidget()` quirk has spread — above the head (298), into the book (303), the homecoming (306), the scan (312) — but always as a fixed fingerprint. The bowl already tracks two transient feelings on a dino: the jealous **sulk** awaiting a make-up greet (`pendingRepair`, 125) and the cold **funk** after a cold night (`coldPending`, 184). This cycle the idle motion reads them: a wandering dino that's sulking shows 😒 above its head in place of its usual quirk — mood over motion, until the keeper makes up with it — and `moodFidget` shades the *label* too ("paces, sulking" / "paces, shivering") for the readouts and hooks. The cut is the laziest correct shape: one pure helper that delegates to `fidget(p)` (no-mood is byte-for-byte the old quirk, so every 298/303/306/312 path stayed green), a `Mood = 'sulk'|'cold'` union, and a one-line shade at the render site reading the *existing* `pendingRepair` state — no new tracking, no save change, no model. Cold keeps its existing floating 🥶 mark, so the live glyph shades only for sulk (no double 🥶). `__moodFidget` dev hook proves the shading through the real scene build. The fed-😋 mood (a one-frame flash, not a lingering state) and feeding the shaded fidget into the book/scan/greeting text are deferred to 318/319/321. 4 unit + 1 e2e new; 698 unit / 225 e2e green, one fresh run, no flake. The bowl's body language has a temper now.
- BACKLOG-309 shipped (structure track) — Stockpile capacity + pressure: the first economy constraint. The gather→bank→craft loop (146/285/286) banked into an *unbounded* per-kind stockpile, so gathering accrued forever with nothing pushing you to spend it. Now each kind caps at 8: a `STOCKPILE_CAP` const, a pure `atCap(pile, kind)`, and a clamping `bankResource` (a kind at cap returns the pile unchanged). In `checkGather`, a pickup of a capped kind is still consumed but banks nothing and logs a beat — `🪵 stores full — Rex drops the branch` — so the pressure to spend reads in-world; the auto-craft (286) that spends a kind drops it below cap and banking resumes. The deliberate design call: the backlog offered "the resource lingers / isn't banked" as an OR, and lingering-on-ground was rejected — a single resource slot, blocked by a stuck capped resource, would stop the *other* kind from ever spawning, so the craft that frees the cap could never happen (a deadlock). "Isn't banked" keeps the world flowing. No save change (the cap is a runtime constant), `stockpileLine` byte-identical, the gather/craft loop (cycle-062/063/064) green. Foundation for 315 (a shelter at a higher threshold). 5 unit + 1 e2e new.

On a live clock crossing into hour 7 (the warm visible dawn) the whole cast greets the day each in its own cycle-44 voice, staggered by `energy` — a pure `audio/chorus.ts` orders the cast highest-energy-first (the eager riser at 0 ms) to lowest-last (the grudging night-owl at the full 1800 ms spread), spacing the rest by where they sit in the cast's energy span, alpha-name tie-break for stability. So the morning rolls across the bowl as a personality read you can hear with your eyes closed, drawn from the same five numbers driving food cravings and den order since cycle 4. WorldScene adds a second live-only `onHour` listener beside the season turn → `checkDawnChorus`: hour-7-gated, once-per-in-game-day on the `lastSeasonDay` template, records the order + a faint 🌅 log line, then schedules each dino's `chirpFor` via `this.time.delayedCall(delayMs)`. `chirpFor` self-guards mute + the unlocked AudioContext, so a muted bowl computes the order and stays silent; `onHour` never fires on `clock.set()`, so boot/restore/away are silent by construction. No new WebAudio (voice.ts unchanged, still the only `AudioContext` file), no deps, no save-format change; NPCBrain never in play. 8 unit + 4 e2e; 10/10 AC; 375 unit / 137 e2e green in a single fresh full run, no flake. Unblocks 196 (night hush) / 197 (chorus you can join) / 198 (off-key loner) / 199 (chorus lead in the book) / 200 (harmonized pair).

### Cycle 44 — 2026-06-11
- BACKLOG-191 shipped — Audio spine: the bowl's first sound is five voices, not a bleep. Pure `audio/chirp.ts` maps the 5 personality axes to synth parameters — pitch from solitariness+timidity (small skittish things squeak high, big social brave things rumble low), call length clipped by energy, pitch-bend from curiosity, pip count from warmth — so the name-seeded founders spread 148 Hz (Mossback) → 343 (Rex) → 513 (Glade) → 521 (Sunny) → 797 (Twitch) with nothing hand-tuned: the voice IS the personality, audible before any text renders. Browser side `audio/voice.ts` is the only WebAudio file: oscillator pips synthesized at master gain 0.12 (desk companion, not a noise box), the AudioContext born strictly inside `unlockAudio()` on the `markActive` first-gesture seam (autoplay-safe on phone Chrome), mute persisted per device under `dino.sound` (M key + a 🔊 row in the touch More sheet). A dino chirps its own voice on the player-greet reply and the dino↔dino convo line; the glass rap got its *thunk*. Hooks record intent in the scene so headless asserts never depend on playback. No assets, no deps, no save change; boundary clean. 7 unit + 5 e2e; 8/8 AC; 361 unit / 131 e2e green (one catalogued parallel flake, green isolated + green fresh run). Unblocks 192 (dawn chorus) / 193 (call-and-answer) / 194 (distress call) / 195 (cry in the book).

### Cycle 41 — 2026-06-10
- BACKLOG-170 shipped — Seasonal palates: the turning year reaches the feeding loop. Each season craves one food (spring greens / summer berries / fall fish / winter meat) and gives it a small +0.4 nudge inside the favorite-food verdict — enough to flip a dino whose top-two foods are near-tied, never enough to move a strong-fit one. So the cast splits on its own: Rex, Mossback, and Sunny each sway with the year (and each to a different food) while Twitch the herbivore and Glade the carnivore stay loyal in all four seasons. The same drop through the hatch now reads the calendar back to you — meat delights a meat-craver in winter, plain feed to that same dino in summer. `season` is optional on `favoriteFood`/`foodReaction`/`scanLines` so omitting it is byte-identical to cycle-061; the rush, the eat, the scan, and the `__favoriteFood` hook all read the same live clock season, which keeps the cycle-027 favorites e2e green by construction. Pure `world/foods.ts`; WorldScene only passes the season in. No save change, no new keys/deps, NPCBrain not in play. 7 unit + 4 e2e; 8/8 AC; 298 unit / 105 e2e green (1 cold-boot flake, green isolated). Unblocks 176 (seasonal gift cravings) + the rest of 174–178.

## Cycle 104 — 2026-07-17 (Milestone 5 "No one goes hungry" — arcs 2 & 2)

- BACKLOG-376 shipped — woke hungry: the need-drive gets a moment. New pure `world/wake.ts` — `wokeHungry(n)` (reads `.hunger` directly vs `NEED_THRESHOLD`, **not** `pressingNeed`, which would drop a hungry-and-thirstier dino's beat), `wakeHungryLine(name, traits)` (temperament-shaded on two axes: prickly → "in no mood about it", high-energy → "already casting about for the hatch", else plaintive; deterministic, total without traits), `wakeHungryMemory()`. `checkWakeHungry()` hangs off the **tail of `checkDawnChorus`**, inheriting its once-per-day + live-only guards rather than duplicating a clock, and runs synchronously (the chorus chirps are staggered via delayedCall; the beat must read the instant dawn breaks). Transient `lastWokeHungry` + `__wokeHungry` hook; no save change. Who wakes hungry is temperament-shaped for free — `needs.ts` scales the hunger rate by energy (0.6×–1.4×). 108 (the dawn stretch) stays open and unblocked; "instead of a plain stretch" shipped as *instead of a plain chorus chirp*. +9 unit, +4 e2e. **Milestone 5 lore arc 2.** (lore track)

The pattern: *a guarantee made for one feature quietly removes a degree of freedom from the next.* (2) *Three seats needs six residents* and the park ships with five, so on a fresh save a council **cannot** outvote its provider — the vote becomes a vote once the ground has grown, which is 031's own "at threshold population" arrived at by arithmetic rather than chosen, and now load-bearing on 479's constants. - [x] BACKLOG-472 [core] The fourth ground — a fourth zone joins the chain as data, and every generalized system meets it untouched.  *(shipped: cycle 119 structure track, Milestone 10 structure arc 1 — the cheque 449 wrote in cycle 108 ("a fourth zone is a row, not three branches") and nobody cashed for eleven cycles. **The Hollow** (`hollow`, "The Hollow"): a damp sink east of the Fernreach, laid out unlike the other three on purpose — a `fern` rim across the north, a standing `water` pool centre-south, no trail — under a cold slate-blue `HOLLOW_TINT`, with `hollowPoolTile` pinned to its own water block by the cycle-108 landmark invariant rather than a comment. Two `ZONE_LINKS` rows appended after the Fernreach's west link so first-match `linkEdge`/`otherZone` behaviour is unchanged; one `ZONES` row; one `ZONE_TERRAIN` row; one `CROP_BY_ZONE` row (a new 🍄 `mushrooms` food + `HOLLOW_PLOT_TILE`); one `CROP_SEASON` row. **Nine cross-zone systems needed zero lines** — prosperity (428), harvest (433), demand (438), pantry (446), ferry (447), provider (448), migration (450), decline (460), governance (463/467/468) — and the Hollow reaches the zone chain, the map lens, the plaque tally and the edge indicators through code written before it existed. The only WorldScene edits were the two per-zone plot maps, now built from `PLOT_TILE_BY_ZONE`'s keys (`emptyPlots`/`emptyPlotStages`) instead of three zone-id literals, plus the additive `hollowPlot` save field. **Four findings, all argued in the open rather than patched:** (1) 465's rotation was written for exactly three crops — the newcomer takes spring, the one free season, and *fall* becomes the park's pinch season carrying two thin crops, because squaring the table would have meant re-pointing roots' lean at spring and breaking the fresh-boot hinge; `seasonCropLine` widened so a two-lean season names both. (2) Per-zone plot persistence is four hand-written save fields — added in the local idiom, generalization logged for a future save-shape item. (3) `ZONE_BIAS`/`structureOf` deliberately left on their back-compat seams (uniform gathering, default cairn); a fourth resource kind drags in recipes, barter, craft escalation and an art rig. (4) **Nine test files hard-coded "the chain is three long"** and every one failed on the fourth row while not one production dispatcher did — the asymmetry that is the honest answer to whether the chain economy was ever general. +16 unit, +3 e2e, 9 files of assertions amended. **Milestone 10 structure arc 1 ✅.** (structure track)*

Cycle 130 ended that reading: two consecutive full runs, 498/499 each, with a **different** victim each time (`cycle-110-plenty`, then `cycle-123-wandering`), neither near that cycle's diff, both green in isolation. At 499 specs the failure is a property of the **run** — contention between parallel workers over the shared dev server, the WebGPU-less browser, and boot — not of any spec's assertions, and the next two-track cycle rolls the same die. Bound the load instead of chasing victims: set an explicit worker cap / shard in `playwright.config.ts` calibrated against a measured run, give the boot path the same settle discipline 456 built for the crossing race, and land the evidence (three consecutive clean full runs) in the QA handoff. Success is a suite whose green is information again. Builds on 456 / 430 / 431. **(2026-08-17, cycle 133: a third distinct victim — `mobile-minds` fell in a full run at 522 specs and passed 5/5 isolated in five seconds.
