## Closed cycle 141 — 2026-08-26

- [x] BACKLOG-300 [social] Caught in the act — interacting (E) with a dino names what it was just doing in its greeting ("caught me mid-snack!", "you pulled me off the pile"), tying the live activity read into dialogue. Builds on 295 / 051.
- [x] BACKLOG-504 [core] The pile is a number in a menu — a ground's banked gathering (285 → 328 per-zone) is now load-bearing in five separate systems: it pays the upkeep bill, it funds a mend, it sets a seat's stake in the ballot (492), it fills toward the granary's raised cap (454), and it is what a courier ferries between grounds (447). The player's only way to see any of that is to open the zone-map lens and read `stockpileLine` — a line of text, in a menu, one screen away from the ground it describes. Nothing is heaped anywhere. A dino carries a stone across the bowl (328's whole point) and it vanishes into an integer. Give the pile a **place**: a per-ground bank tile the gathering visibly accumulates on, whose rendered heap grows in steps with `pileTotal` and shrinks when the pile is spent, so the upkeep bill being paid and the granary filling are things that happen *somewhere the player is standing* rather than things a menu reports afterward. The rendering rides the existing prop path (`bakePropArt`, per-item fallback); the item is the **placement + the step function**, not the art. This is the CHARTER v7 bar applied to the park's oldest structural loop: it has been true and unwatchable since cycle 285. Builds on 285 / 328 / 446 / 454 / 492.
- [x] BACKLOG-506 [art] The pile has no heap — BACKLOG-504 gives a ground's banked gathering a **place** (a bank tile whose rendered heap steps with `pileTotal`), and the thing that is supposed to be standing on that tile does not exist. Author a **stacked-gathering prop rig at three fullness steps** — `pile_1` a scatter of two or three stones barely off the grass, `pile_2` a proper shouldered heap with a stone or two fallen off the side, `pile_3` a high cairn-adjacent stack that reads full at a glance from across a ground — baked through `bakePropArt` and keyed so the step function can swap one for the next as the bank fills and empties. Park earth tones, not the keeper palette: this is a **dino-made** heap of what dinos carried, so it should read as piled rather than built, with the fallen-off stones doing the work of saying nobody stacked this carefully. It must be legible at a glance in *silhouette* — the whole point is that a keeper standing on the Grove can tell a full bank from a spent one without opening the lens. Each step renders standalone against a grass swatch (cycle-91 stash-ahead rule cleared), and a missing step simply draws nothing — the same per-item fallback 490, 494, 496 and 502 all ship. Builds on 504 / 328 / 296 / 490.

## Closed cycle 142 (2026-08-27)

> Three items, and the shape of the night is in which stage closed which. The chain shipped 503 and 507;

- [x] BACKLOG-503 [core] The branch with nothing to choose — `zoneChain()` walks the grounds east in a line, and exactly one of them, the Ridge, hangs off a **branch**: the only ground in this park a player reaches by deciding to rather than by continuing to. That is the most interesting piece of map geometry the studio has built and it is spent on nothing. The Ridge grows what the line grows, banks what the line banks, and offers no resource, crop, landmark or role that the walk cannot supply — so the branch is a longer route to the same place, and every migration heuristic that weighs it (450's prosperity pull, 340's homesickness, the settle-resist) is choosing between two identical arguments. Give the branch a **stake**: one thing that exists on the Ridge and genuinely nowhere else — a zone-exclusive gatherable, or a crop that only ripens there — so a dino that needs it must *go*, and a keeper who wants it must *climb*. This is the operator's own Idea Box nudge (2026-07-18), which cycle 106 routed here with the instruction to weigh it "when 450 is built": a step past 450's soft prosperity **bias** to a hard scarcity **pull**. Pairs with 500 — 500 puts a resident on the Ridge, this gives the resident somewhere to be *from*. Builds on 450 / 340 / 285 / 328.

- [x] BACKLOG-496 [art] **(3 of 3 drawn — closed cycle 142-art. Previously: 2 of 3 drawn — the two-tile scuff and the trodden ring shipped cycle 138-art; `fuss` stays undrawn as the per-kind fallback control, and the world wiring that lays one under a tic anchor is still to come)** The ritual's little path — the tic (405) is the most per-dino behaviour in the park and it leaves the ground exactly as it found it: a dino paces the same two tiles for a hundred steps, turns the same slow circle, fusses the same patch, and the grass is untouched. Author a **worn-ground prop rig per `TicKind`** — a two-tile scuff for `pace`, a trodden ring for `circle`, a disturbed patch of loose earth for `fuss` — baked through `bakePropArt` and keyed `tic_<kind>`, so `WorldScene` can lay one under a dino's tic anchor and lift it when the stretch ends. Each renders standalone against a grass swatch (no host terrain), so it clears the cycle-91 stash-ahead rule, and a kind with no rig simply draws nothing — the same per-item fallback 490 and 494 both ship. With 421 queued to make the anchor *drift*, the mark is what turns a drifting anchor into a visible little path. Builds on 405 / 407 / 421 / 296.

- [x] BACKLOG-508 [art] The black glass and the thing built from it — cycle 142 gives the Ridge the only resource in the park that exists on exactly one ground (BACKLOG-503): **obsidian**, and the **beacon** raised from it. Both ship on the glyph fallback, which is the fourth resource in a park where the other three (`branch`, `stone`, `frond`) all bake a pixel rig, and the fifth landmark where the other four (cairn, lean-to, thatch, granary) all do. Author two prop rigs: `obsidian` — a shard of volcanic glass, and the first prop in `PROP_RIGS` whose palette is genuinely *dark*, so it must read against grass by silhouette and a single specular edge rather than by hue; and `beacon` — the Ridge's landmark, shards set upright in a stack so it reads as *built* rather than fallen, and tall enough to be the one skyline in the park you can see is different from across a ground. Both render standalone against a grass swatch (cycle-91 stash rule cleared), and a rig-less build draws the 🌑 / 🗼 glyph — the same per-item fallback 490, 494, 496, 502 and 506 all ship. Builds on 503 / 296 / 506.

- [x] BACKLOG-507 [emergent] The ritual's mark, laid on the ground — 496 drew the worn ground (`tic_pace`, `tic_circle` baked and registered since cycle 138-art) and **nothing in the world blits it**. A dino paces the same two tiles for a hundred steps and the grass is untouched, because the rigs exist only in `PROP_RIGS`. Meanwhile 421 has been persisting, per dino per ground, the exact datum the mark wants: `ticHaunts` — a remembered tile that the dino returns to and that drifts one step every stretch performed there. Lay the mark on the haunt. A worn patch is drawn under every haunt on the ground the keeper is standing on, keyed `tic_<kind>` off the dino's own signature (or echoed) tic, moving as the haunt drifts, so a habit that has wandered four tiles leaves a *little path* of worn ground rather than one static smudge. A kind with no rig draws nothing — `fuss` is deliberately undrawn and is the per-kind fallback control 496 reserved. Grief anchors (414) are excluded: a dino pacing the edge its friend left by is not keeping a habit and lays no path. Builds on 405 / 421 / 496 / 504 (`syncBank` is the sprite-or-glyph shape this copies).

## Closed cycle 143 — the mouth and the far end (2026-08-28)

> Five bullets closed in one night (two of them the same item's pointer and body), and for the second cycle
> running the pair that closed together was a wiring item split out of an `[art]` item plus the `[art]` item
> itself. Two whole sections emptied and came with them, preserved below with their blockquotes intact. The
> `## Art` section stayed in the working backlog although it too emptied: its body carries the cycle-91
> stash-ahead rule, which is standing policy the Artist routine reads, not a spent queue.

- [x] BACKLOG-505 [core] The frontier 500 closed — putting a resident on every ground made 474's unsettled-ground read, its lens glyph and the frontier migration tier dormant on a fresh save (full text in the cycle-140 block below).
- [x] BACKLOG-502 [art] The hatch has no mouth — `H` drops food and the park's whole social engine runs on it (the swarm, the cede, the gobble, the pecking order, the errand that brings a loner to it, four remembered beats and a Park News line), and there is **nothing on screen where it happens**. Food materialises on bare grass; `dropFood` picks a tile near the keeper and no object marks the spot before or after. Every other prop the player looks at bakes a pixel rig; the single most-used interaction in the game is rendered by its absence. Author a `hatch` pixel prop rig — a keeper-made thing, not a dino-made one, so it should read as the odd artefact among the reed and plaster landmarks: dark metal rim set flush into the ground, a shadowed opening, and the faint seam of a shutter, in the keeper palette rather than the park's earth tones. It renders standalone against a grass swatch (cycle-91 stash rule cleared), keys `hatch` in `PROP_RIGS`, and a rig-less build simply draws nothing — the same per-item fallback 490, 494 and 496 all ship. The world wiring that fixes a hatch position per ground and lays the sprite under it is a follow-up, not this item. Builds on 059 / 296 / 490.
- [x] BACKLOG-505 [core] The frontier 500 closed — BACKLOG-474 built the *unsettled ground*: a ground nobody lives on reads unsettled, carries its own lens glyph, and is what the frontier migration tier aims a wanderer at over a richer inhabited neighbour. CHARTER v7's third change says there must be no such ground. So 500, by obeying the constitution, took a system that was reachable on a fresh save and made it dormant on one — `__unsettled()` returns `[]` at boot where it returned `['hollow', 'ridge']` — which is the exact shape of defect v7's own corollary was written against, arrived at from the other direction. `cycle-120-unsettled.spec.ts` says so out loud rather than being quietly reworded, and proves the read still fires the instant a ground actually empties; two of its three tests now *make* a frontier by walking the residents out first. Decide what the frontier is now *for* in a park with no empty ground, and give it a reachable form: the honest candidates are a **sixth ground** (a frontier that exists because it has not been settled yet, which is what 474 always meant), or re-pointing the tier at a ground that has *lost* its last resident (the read is already heads-not-history, so the machinery is there). Do not resolve it by loosening the residency invariant — that is the constitution. Pairs with 501, which is the instrument that should have caught this rather than a spec. Builds on 500 / 474 / 478 / 343.
- [x] BACKLOG-510 [core] The hatch gets a mouth — the wiring half of BACKLOG-502, split out because 502 defers it in its own text and nothing else may build it. `H` is the most-pressed key in this park and the food it drops falls **out of the sky onto bare grass**: `dropFood` picks a random column, spawns the piece at `y = TILE * 0.4` — above the top of the world — and tweens it down to a landing tile that is different every time and marked by nothing before or after. Every social system in the game hangs off that landing (the swarm, the cede, the gobble, the pecking order, the escort that fetches a loner to it, four remembered beats and a Park News line) and the place it happens does not exist. Give each ground a **fixed hatch tile**, the `BANK_TILE` treatment (504) applied to the feeding hatch: one tile, the same on every ground so the player learns one place and can then find the hatch anywhere — exactly the argument `H` being one key everywhere already makes — grass on all six grounds and clear of every fixture the park pins, asserted against `zoneTileAt` for every ground in `zoneChain()` rather than promised in a comment. The sprite is laid under it at boot on the same per-item fallback 490/494/496/504 all ship: `hatch` bakes if 502 has drawn it, and the glyph stands in until it has, so the wiring is never blocked on the art and the art is never blocked on the wiring. And the drop **comes out of the hatch**: the piece emerges at the hatch tile and travels to its landing rather than falling from off-screen, which is the read the whole item is for — food no longer materialises out of nowhere. The landing keeps its randomness (the escort, the berth and the rush all read distance to it, and a fixed landing would flatten every one of them) but scatters *around the hatch column* instead of across the whole map, and keeps its row, so the feeding row every existing spec pins does not move. Builds on 502 / 504 / 490 / 381.
- [x] BACKLOG-511 [art] The salt crust — the sixth ground (BACKLOG-505, this cycle) ships a new `salt` tile kind for its cracked pan floor, and like `path`/`water` at 294 and `fern` at 399 it bakes as grass under the ground's tint until a rig exists. Draw it. This is the first terrain kind in the park whose host ships in the *same* cycle as the request, so the cycle-91 stash rule is not in play — the ground to paint on is already there. It should read as the anti-grass: a pale, dry, hard crust broken into irregular plates by dark hairline cracks, no blades and no soft edges, so that walking from the Hollow's fen onto it is a change the player sees in the floor rather than only in the tint. Keys `salt` in the terrain rig table beside `FERN_RIG`, and a rig-less build still bakes grass — the fallback that has kept the floor whole through four terrain additions is untouched. Builds on 399 / 294 / 505.

## Closed cycle 144 — the park learns to say its own place names, and a founding is written down (2026-08-29)

> Both tracks APPROVED. 499 replaced nineteen hand-rolled article decisions with one seam and left a
> repo-wide grep test where two source comments used to warn the next author — and found, on the way,
> that `cycle-138-billcall.spec.ts` had been asserting the doubled article **verbatim**, so the defect
> was not merely shipped for seven cycles, it was pinned by a green spec looking at something else.
> 512 deleted a special case rather than adding four more: `foundingPioneers()` records a founding as a
> founding, `isOrigin` comes out, and an emptied ground reads *hollowed* and names who settled it.

- [x] BACKLOG-499 [core] The ground with two articles — every governance beat prints "the The Grove's council calls it", because `ZONES` carries the article in each ground's display name (`The Grove`, `The Hollow`) and 481's template prepends another. Cosmetic, pre-existing since 481, and visible on a fresh save the first time any ground announces anything — which since 488 and 492 is *within the first step*. The fix is one decision made once (either the names lose their articles and every template supplies one, or the templates stop supplying one), not a `slice` at the call site: there are now six lines that interpolate a zone name — the two council calls, the bill call, the discontent grumble, the handover beat and the lens legend — and patching the loudest one is how the second article got there in the first place. Found by QA reading the exact string a cycle-138 spec asserts. Builds on 481 / 485 / 487.
- [x] BACKLOG-512 [core] The frontier read calls lived-in ground unlived-in — `isUnsettled` (474) is "no residents **and** no pioneer **and** not the origin", and its `isOrigin` clause names exactly one ground: the bowl, because 343 records a pioneer at *arrival* and nothing records one at spawn. That was true and harmless while the bowl was the only ground anybody spawned on. CHARTER v7's spread cast (486) and BACKLOG-500 changed it: Bramble and Pip wake in the Grove, Murk in the Hollow, Ember on the Ridge, and **not one of those grounds has a pioneer**. So the moment any of them empties — a migration, a crowding exodus, a single resident walking out — the park declares a ground its cast has lived on since the first frame to be a place nobody has ever seen, lights the frontier badge on it, and aims the migration tier at it over an inhabited neighbour. `cycle-143-saltpan.spec.ts` pins the behaviour out loud (walk the Saltpan's founder back out and the Hollow starts reading unsettled) rather than asserting around it. Note the shape: this is BACKLOG-505's *second* candidate — "re-point the tier at a ground that has lost its last resident" — already half-implemented by accident, and pointing the wrong way, since it fires on grounds that were never lost so much as never recorded. The honest fix is to record a founding as a founding: seed a pioneer for every ground the roster spawns on, so `isOrigin` stops being a special case for one id and "nobody has ever lived here" becomes a claim about history rather than about which ground the save happens to call home. Then an emptied ground reads *hollowed* (460), which is what it is, and 505's second candidate becomes buildable on purpose instead of true by accident. Pairs with 501, which is the instrument that should surface this class before a spec does. Builds on 505 / 500 / 486 / 474 / 460 / 343.
